lps = [lp for lp in lps if np.isfinite(lp)]\\n    return float(np.mean(lps)) if lps else float(\\\"nan\\\")\\n\\ndef safe_spearman(x, y):\\n    x, y = np.asarray(x, float), np.asarray(y, float)\\n    m = np.isfinite(x) & np.isfinite(y)\\n    x, y = x[m], y[m]\\n    if len(x) < 3: return float(\\\"nan\\\"), float(\\\"nan\\\")\\n    r = spearmanr(x, y)\\n    return float(r.statistic), float(r.pvalue)\\n\\ndef safe_auroc(scores, labels):\\n    scores, labels = np.asarray(scores, float), np.asarray(labels, int)\\n    if len(np.unique(labels)) < 2: return float(\\\"nan\\\")\\n    n_pos, n_neg = (labels==1).sum(), (labels==0).sum()\\n    if n_pos == 0 or n_neg == 0: return float(\\\"nan\\\")\\n    ranks = rankdata(scores)\\n    u = ranks[labels==1].sum() - n_pos*(n_pos+1)/2\\n    return float(u / (n_pos * n_neg))\\n\\ndef compute_ece(scores, labels, n_bins=10):\\n    scores, labels = np.asarray(scores, float), np.asarray(labels, float)\\n    mask = np.isfinite(scores)\\n    scores, labels = scores[mask], labels[mask]\\n    if len(scores) == 0: return float(\\\"nan\\\")\\n    s_min, s_max = scores.min(), scores.max()\\n    if s_max == s_min: return float(\\\"nan\\\")\\n    norm = (scores - s_min) / (s_max - s_min)\\n    ece = 0.0\\n    for i in range(n_bins):\\n        lo, hi = i / n_bins, (i + 1) / n_bins\\n        mask_bin = (norm >= lo) & (norm < hi) if i < n_bins - 1 else (norm >= lo) & (norm <= hi)\\n        if mask_bin.sum() == 0: continue\\n        avg_conf = norm[mask_bin].mean()\\n        avg_acc = labels[mask_bin].mean()\\n        ece += mask_bin.sum() / greedy_f1s) if f1 > 0]\\n\\n    results = {\\\"n_total\\\": len(instances), \\\"n_valid\\\": len(valid), \\\"n_conditional\\\": len(conditional)}\\n    \\n    for split_name, split_insts in cons[\\\"fleiss_kappa\\\"]\\n        \\n        lp, em, vc, f1s = [], [], [], []\\n        for inst in split_insts:\\n            samples = inst[\\\"samples\\\"]\\n            greedy = Let me verify this against the existing report.json which already has `ner_logprob_baseline.full.auroc = 0.6116` to make sure my approach is correct. So if I use the ">0" convention, I'd have 477 positive instances and 52 negative ones, but the conditional evaluation only includes cases where greedy F1 is already positive, making AUROC undefined since there's no variation in the labels. Looking at the existing code, it seems to use F1 >= 1.0 for the binary classification instead, which would give a proper mix of positive and negative cases for computing AUROC.

Fleiss' Kappa — surface-level entity agreement\\n# ===================================================================\\n\\ndef _extract_surface_keys(sample: Extraction, subtask: str) -> set[tuple]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Extract surface-level keys from a sample for Fleiss' Kappa computation.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    if subtask == \\\"ner\\\":\\n        return {(e[\\\"text\\\"], e[\\\"type\\\"]) for e in sample.get(\\\"entities\\\", [])}\\n    elif subtask == \\\"re\\\":\\n        return {(r[\\\"head\\\"], r[\\\"tail\\\"], r[\\\"type\\\"]) for r in sample.get(\\\"relations\\\", [])}\\n    elif subtask == \\\"eae\\\":\\n        keys: set[tuple] = set()\\n        for ev in sample.get(\\\"events\\\", []):\\n            t = ev.get(\\\"trigger\\\", {})\\n            for arg in ev.get(\\\"arguments\\\", []):\\n                keys.add((t.get(\\\"text\\\", \\\"\\\"), t.get(\\\"type\\\", \\\"\\\"), arg[\\\"role\\\"], arg[\\\"text\\\"]))\\n        return keys\\n    else:\\n        raise ValueError(f\\\"Unknown subtask: {subtask}\\\")\\n\\n\\ndef fleiss_kappa_surface(samples: list[Extraction], subtask: str = \\\"ner\\\") -> float:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Compute Fleiss' kappa treating N LLM samples as N raters on item presence.\\n\\n    Items are subtask-dependent surface keys:\\n    - ner: (entity_text, entity_type)\\n    - re: (head_text, tail_text, relation_type)\\n    - eae: (trigger_text, trigger_type, role, argument_text)\\n\\n    Args:\\n        samples: List of N extraction dicts.\\n        subtask: \\\"ner\\\", \\\"re\\\", or \\\"eae\\\".\\n\\n    Returns:\\n        Fleiss' kappa in [-1, 1].  Returns 1.0 for degenerate cases (<=1 unique\\n        item, perfect agreement, or undefined P_e).\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    n_raters = len(samples)\\n    if n_raters <= 1:\\n        return 1.0\\n\\n    # Collect unique keys and per-sample presence sets\\n    entity_sets: list[set[tuple]] = []\\n    all_keys: set[tuple] = set()\\n    for sample in samples:\\n        keys = _extract_surface_keys(sample, subtask)\\n        entity_sets.append(keys)\\n        all_keys |= keys\\n\\n    n_subjects = len(all_keys)\\n    if n_subjects <= 0:\\n        # All samples extracted nothing — perfect agreement on empty set\\n        return 1.0\\n\\n    key_list = sorted(all_keys)\\n\\n    # Build rating matrix: n_subjects x 2 (categories: absent=0, present=1)\\n    # n_ij = number of raters assigning subject i to category j\\n    rating = np.zeros((n_subjects, 2), dtype=np.int64)\\n    for es in entity_sets:\\n        for idx, key in enumerate(key_list):\\n            if key in es:\\n                rating[idx, 1] += 1\\n            else:\\n                rating[idx, 0] += 1\\n\\n    n = n_raters\\n    k = 2  # number of categories\\n\\n    # Check perfect agreement: every subject has all votes in one category\\n    if np.all(np.max(rating, axis=1) == n):\\n        return 1.0\\n\\n    # P_i for each subject\\n    P_i = (np.sum(rating ** 2, axis=1) - n) / (n * (n - 1))\\n    P_bar = np.mean(P_i)\\n\\n    # P_e — expected agreement by chance\\n    p_j = np.sum(rating, axis=0) / (n_subjects * n)\\n    P_e = np.sum(p_j ** 2)\\n\\n    if abs(1.0 - P_e) < 1e-12:\\n\\n... \\\"exp_007_llama_n16\\\": {\\n        \\\"path\\\": \\\"/root/autodl-tmp/struct_self_consist_ie/output/exp_007_llama_n16/samples.jsonl\\\",\\n        \\\"subtask\\\": \\\"ner\\\",\\n        \\\"dataset\\\": \\\"scierc\\\",\\n    },\\n}\\n\\ndef load_data(path):\\n    with open(path) as f:\\n        return [json.loads(line) for line in f if line.strip()]\\n\\ndef compute_exact_match_rate(samples, subtask):\\n    if subtask == \\\"ner\\\":\\n        keys = [frozenset((e[\\\"text\\\"], e[\\\"type\\\"]) for e in s.get(\\\"entities\\\", [])) for s in samples]\\n    else:\\n        keys = [frozenset((r[\\\"head\\\"], r[\\\"tail\\\"], r[\\\"type\\\"]) for r in s.get(\\\"relations\\\", [])) for s in samples]\\n    if not keys: return 0.0\\n    c = Counter(keys)\\n    return c.most_common(1)[0][1] / len(samples)\\n\\ndef compute_voting_confidence(samples, subtask):\\n    N = len(samples)\\n    if N == 0: return 0.0\\n    counter = Counter()\\n    if subtask == \\\"ner\\\":\\n        for s in samples:\\n            for e in s.get(\\\"entities\\\", []):\\n                counter[(e[\\\"text\\\"], e[\\\"type\\\"])] += 1\\n    else:\\n        for s in samples:\\n            for r in s.get(\\\"relations\\\", []):\\n                counter[(r[\\\"head\\\"], r[\\\"tail\\\"], r[\\\"type\\\"])] += 1\\n    if not counter: return 0.0\\n    return float(np.mean([v / N for v in counter.values()]))\\n\\ndef compute_mean_logprob(samples):\\n    lps = [s.get(\\\"mean_logprob\\\") for s in samples if s.get(\\\"mean_logprob\\\") is not None]\\n    lps = [lp for lp in lps if np.isfinite(lp)]\\n    return float(np.mean(lps)) if lps else float(\\\"nan\\\")\\n\\ndef safe_spearman(x, y):\\n    x, y = np.asarray(x, float), np.asarray(y, float)\\n    m = np.isfinite(x) & np.isfinite(y)\\n    x, y = x[m], y[m]\\n    if len(x) < 3: return float(\\\"nan\\\"), float(\\\"nan\\\")\\n    r = spearmanr(x, y)\\n    return float(r.statistic), float(r.pvalue)\\n\\ndef safe_auroc(scores, labels):\\n    scores, labels = np.asarray(scores, float), np.asarray(labels, int)\\n    if len(np.unique(labels)) < 2: return float(\\\"nan\\\")\\n    n_pos, n_neg = (labels==1).sum(), (labels==0).sum()\\n    if n_pos == 0 or n_neg == 0: return float(\\\"nan\\\")\\n    ranks = rankdata(scores)\\n    u = ranks[labels==1].sum() - n_pos*(n_pos+1)/2\\n    return float(u / (n_pos * n_neg))\\n\\ndef compute_ece(scores, labels, n_bins=10):\\n    scores, labels = np.asarray(scores, float), np.asarray(labels, float)\\n    mask = np.isfinite(scores)\\n    scores, labels = scores[mask], labels[mask]\\n    if len(scores) == 0: return float(\\\"nan\\\")\\n    s_min, s_max = scores.min(), scores.max()\\n    if s_max == s_min: return float(\\\"nan\\\")\\n    norm = (scores - s_min) / (s_max - s_min)\\n    ece = 0.0\\n    for i in range(n_bins):\\n        lo, hi = i / n_bins, (i + 1) / n_bins\\n        mask_bin = (norm >= lo) & (norm < hi) if i < n_bins - 1 else (norm >= lo) & (norm <= hi)\\n        if mask_bin.sum() == 0: continue\\n        avg_conf = norm[mask_bin].mean()\\n        avg_acc = labels[mask_bin].mean()\\n        ece += mask_bin.sum() / len(norm) * abs(avg_conf - avg_acc)\\n    return float(ece)\\n\\ndef analyze(path, subtask):\\n    instances = load_data(path)\\n    entity_key = \\\"entities\\\" if subtask == \\\"ner\\\" else \\\"relations\\\"\\n    valid = [inst for inst in instances if len(inst[\\\"gold\\\"].get(entity_key, [])) > 0]\\n\\n    greedy_f1s = []\\n    for inst in valid:\\n        greedy = inst.get(\\\"greedy\\\", inst[\\\"samples\\\"][0])\\n        greedy_f1s.append(per_instance_f1(greedy, inst[\\\"gold\\\"], subtask=subtask))\\n    conditional = [inst for inst, f1 in zip(valid, greedy_f1s) if f1 > 0]\\n\\n    results = {\\\"n_total\\\": len(instances), \\\"n_valid\\\": len(valid), \\\"n_conditional\\\": len(conditional)}\\n    \\n    for split_name, split_insts in [(\\\"full\\\", valid), (\\\"conditional\\\", conditional)]:\\n        cons = compute_all_consistency_scores(split_insts, subtask=subtask)\\n        sj = cons[\\\"soft_jaccard\\\"]\\n        fk = cons[\\\"fleiss_kappa\\\"]\\n        \\n        lp, em, vc, f1s = [], [], [], []\\n        for inst in split_insts:\\n            samples = inst[\\\"samples\\\"]\\n            greedy = inst.get(\\\"greedy\\\", samples[0])\\n            lp.append(compute_mean_logprob(samples))\\n            em.append(compute_exact_match_rate(samples, subtask))\\n            vc.append(compute_voting_confidence(samples, subtask))\\n            f1s.append(per_instance_f1(greedy, inst[\\\"gold\\\"], subtask=subtask))\\n        \\n        signals = {\\\"SJ\\\": np.array(sj), \\\"FK\\\": np.array(fk), \\\"logprob\\\": np.array(lp),\\n                    \\\"EM\\\": np.array(em), \\\"voting_conf\\\": np.array(vc)}\\n        f1_arr = np.array(f1s)\\n        binary = (f1_arr >= 1.0).astype(int)\\n        \\n        split_res = {\\\"n\\\": len(split_insts)}\\n        for name, vals in signals.items():\\n            rho, p_rho = safe_spearman(vals, f1_arr)\\n            auroc = safe_auroc(vals, binary)\\n            ece = compute_ece(vals, f1_arr)\\n            split_res[name] = {\\\"rho\\\": round(rho, 4), \\\"p_rho\\\": p_rho, \\\"auroc\\\": round(auroc, 4), \\\"ece\\\": round(ece, 4)}\\n        \\n        split_res[\\\"greedy_f1_mean\\\"] = round(float(np.mean(f1s)), 4)\\n        results[split_name] = split_res\\n    return results\\n\\nall_results = {}\\nfor exp_id, cfg in EXPERIMENTS.items():\\n    print(f\\\"Processing {exp_id} ({cfg['dataset']})...\\\")\\n    all_results[exp_id] = analyze(cfg[\\\"path\\\"], cfg[\\\"subtask\\\"])\\n    all_results[exp_id][\\\"dataset\\\"] = cfg[\\\"dataset\\\"]\\n\\nout_path = \\\"/root/autodl-tmp/struct_self_consist_ie/output/n16_5signal_results.json\\\"\\nwith open(out_path, \\\"w\\\") as f:\\n ```text
The conditional split with binary = (f1 > 0) has all positives (n_pos=477, n_neg=0 for NER and n_pos=238, n_neg=0 for RE), so AUROC is undefined (all are positive). This makes sense — conditional filters out f1=0, and if binary is >0, then all remaining are positive.

For the conditional split to be meaningful, we need to use binary = (f1 >= 1.0) instead. This is what the existing eval scripts do. Then conditional (f1 > 0) gives a mix of "perfect match" (F1=1.0) and "partial match" (0 < F1 < 1.0).


I'm re-reading the task requirements — it specifies computing metrics for both full and conditional (excluding greedy_F1=0) splits. The issue is that using f1 > 0 as the binary threshold creates a contradiction for AUROC since all remaining instances after filtering would be positive. I should stick with f1 >= 1.0 as the binary threshold, which aligns with the existing eval scripts and produces meaningful results for both splits.

Updating the script to use this binarization convention...

   else:\n        keys = [frozenset((r[\"head\"], r[\"tail\"], r[\"type\"]) for r in s.get(\"relations\", [])) for s in samples]\n    if not keys: return 0.0\n    c = Counter(keys)\n    return
